# Independent PIM control: every changed line explained

This guide explains commit **3b98807** against its parent: **1,624 added lines and 9 removed lines across 23 files**. It includes imported benchmark code, configuration, documentation, and saved measurements, not just the newly written controller. Nothing in this guide changes the simulator's behavior.

Every changed line appears below with its exact text, original/new line number, and a plain-language explanation. Blank lines, braces, imports, and removed lines are included. Explanations occasionally describe a small multi-line operation together, but every individual line still has its own entry. Unchanged surrounding lines are omitted.

Numbers refer to this commit. If the implementation is edited later, current editor line numbers may move. `+` means added in that commit; `−` means removed from the parent. “Imported” marks lines copied from the existing sparse benchmark branch, not newly invented behavior. For removed lines, the old line number is informational rather than a link into the current file.

## Start here: what problem is this code modeling?

A sparse matrix has many zero positions. In matrix-vector multiplication, each matrix row produces one output number by multiplying its entries with matching vector elements and adding them together. Consider:

```text
A = [0  2  0  3]    x = [10, 20, 30, 40]
    [4  0  0  0]

Output row 0 = 2*20 + 3*40 = 160
Output row 1 = 4*10        = 40
```

**CSR (compressed sparse row)** stores just the nonzeros plus instructions for finding them:

```text
values  = [2, 3, 4]      actual numbers
col_idx = [1, 3, 0]      which x element to multiply each number by
row_ptr = [0, 2, 3]      row 0 occupies entries [0,2); row 1 occupies [2,3)
```

Array indices start at zero. `[0,2)` means indices 0 and 1, excluding 2. The final row pointer is needed so the last row has an end too. An empty row has equal start and end pointers. A multiply-and-accumulate (**MAC**) means `sum = sum + a*x`. The implicit zero positions omitted by CSR never enter this loop; a zero explicitly present in `values` would still execute a MAC.

If two blocks own rows of different lengths, their work differs. A **lockstep** policy makes them wait at matching iteration/row boundaries. **Independent** control lets a block move on when its own operands and result are ready. This does not let it ignore its own dependencies or bypass memory timing. The implementation assumes rows are independent; it does not discover dependencies in arbitrary programs.

## What the simulator and test each do

This repository is software that models hardware. A field like `waiting` represents hardware control state; the C++ program itself executes on your computer. “Device-controlled” here means that code under `src/`, advanced by the simulator's clock, chooses progress. Code under `src/tests/` prepares work, starts the model, waits, and checks results.

The complete interaction is:

```text
Test:       generate matrix -> choose static row ownership -> load memory
            -> submit one job -> keep advancing simulation time -> read/check output

Controller: for each block, independently:
            read row start -> read row end -> read column -> read value -> read x
            -> MAC -> repeat nonzeros -> write row result -> next row -> done

Memory:     queue requests -> issue legal DRAM commands -> transfer data
            -> run completion callback -> controller can advance
```

The **PC** (program counter) in this extension is a named instruction state such as `Column`, `Input`, or `Mac`. Every block has its own state. It is **not** a private instruction index for the old CRF instruction stream. The original `PIMRank` CRF/SIMD interpreter retains its shared PC; the extension is a separate scalar FP32 CSR control/datapath model.

A **job descriptor** is a data structure saying where arrays are and which rows each block owns. It does not specify issue times. The default fixture statically assigns rows round-robin. An **accumulator** stores the partial sum of a row. **FP32** means a 32-bit floating-point number. A **burst** is one memory transfer, 32 bytes here, containing eight 4-byte words. A **lane** identifies the desired word inside that burst.

A **channel** has its own memory controller. A **rank** contains banks, and this architecture associates one PIM block with a pair of banks. Lockstep barriers are scoped to one channel/rank. A **barrier** is a meeting point where a block waits for peers. **Backpressure** means a request queue is full, so the issuer must retry later.

Memory is asynchronous: requesting a read does not immediately return the value. A **callback** is a saved function to invoke when it finishes. Every block keeps one persistent burst buffer and at most one outstanding request. It waits for completion before using or overwriting that buffer. The controller then advances its PC; there is no host instruction saying “run block 3 now.” A configured MAC latency adds arithmetic waiting as well.

## What is a fair baseline here?

The control-only comparison uses the **new sparse lockstep policy** and the **new sparse independent policy**. They share matrix contents, ownership, placement, arithmetic order, and MAC latency. Synchronization is the intended difference. This is not a direct performance comparison with the old SIMD CRF implementation.

The original host benchmark remains available, but it measures HBM service traffic, not CPU computation. Its cycles therefore do not establish end-to-end CPU SpMV speed. The PIM executor currently uses CSR for every case, even when an original case requests COO/ELL/BCSR; both requested and executed format are printed. It conservatively sends operand traffic through the existing controller/data-bus path rather than modeling a new bank-to-PIM interconnect. Teammates' gather/mapping features need explicit integration with this path; they are not automatically combined just because their files are merged.

## Energy and power in plain terms

A speed improvement is fewer simulated execution cycles. **Energy** is the total cost of the run; **power** is energy consumed per unit time. Finishing sooner can increase average power if more work occurs per unit time.

The old energy counters were not being populated. The new estimate counts actual issued reads/writes/row activations/precharges/refreshes, then multiplies by configurable assumed costs. Activation opens a DRAM row; precharge prepares a bank for a new row; refresh maintains DRAM contents. It also charges MAC/instruction costs and continuous rank/block power, including extra independent-control power.

```text
Dynamic energy = sum(event count * energy per event)
Static energy  = runtime * continuously consumed power
Average power  = total energy / runtime
```

`pJ` means picojoules, `ns` nanoseconds, and `mW` milliwatts. Conveniently, pJ/ns = mW, and ns*mW = pJ. The supplied coefficient values are **illustrative and uncalibrated**. They exercise the estimate; they were not measured or derived from this hardware. Counts/timing can be inspected directly, but hardware power claims require supported coefficients and further model calibration.

Setup loading and validation readback are outside the timed PIM execution region. The code takes cumulative command snapshots before and after execution and subtracts them, rather than counting those extra transfers as computation.

## Small C++/script syntax dictionary

| Syntax | Meaning in this code |
| --- | --- |
| `struct` / `class` | A bundle of named data fields and/or functions; per-block state is one such bundle. |
| `uint32_t`, `uint64_t` | Unsigned integers with 32 or 64 bits; used for indices, counters, and addresses. |
| `float`, `double` | Floating-point numbers; row arithmetic uses float, energy estimates use double. |
| `std::vector<T>` | A resizable list of items of type T; brackets select an item. |
| `auto` | Let the compiler infer a variable's type from the assigned expression. |
| `auto& b = ...` | Give an existing object a shorter name without copying it; edits to b affect the original. |
| `const` | This reference/method exposes data for reading, not modification through that reference. |
| `*` / `&` | Depending on position: pointer/dereference or reference/address-of. A pointer locates an existing object. |
| `object.field` / `pointer->field` | Read a field directly, or through a pointer. |
| `::` | Qualifies which namespace or class a name belongs to. |
| `std::function<void()>` | A stored callable taking no arguments and returning no value. Here it notifies a completion. |
| `[this, id, ...]() { ... }` | A lambda: a small function that remembers the listed values until it runs later. |
| `if`, `else`, `for`, `while`, `switch` | Choose a branch or repeat work; a switch selects an action based on the PC. |
| `return` / `break` | Leave the function / leave the current loop or switch case. |
| `throw` | Stop normal execution with an error; prevents silently accepting invalid model inputs. |
| `++x` / `x++` | Add one; prefix uses the new value, postfix uses the old value in that expression. |
| `%` | Remainder, used for round-robin ownership or selecting a word within a burst. |
| `&&`, `\|\|`, `!` | Logical AND, OR, and NOT. |
| `condition ? a : b` | Choose a when the condition is true, otherwise b. |
| `<<` / `\|` in addresses | Shift bits to make space / combine non-overlapping bit fields. In cout, << instead means append output. |
| `sizeof(T)` | Number of bytes an object of type T occupies. |
| `4ULL`, `1U`, `0.5F` | Suffixes select unsigned integer widths or floating-point types; they are not extra arithmetic operations. |
| `#include` / header guards | Compile-time declarations/import protection; not simulated instructions or time costs. |
| `ASSERT_*` / `EXPECT_*` | GoogleTest checks. An assertion failure stops that test path; an expectation records failure and continues. |
| `${VAR:-default}` | Bash: use the variable's value or use the fallback if unset/empty. |
| `$(command)` | Bash: capture the command's printed output into an expression. |

<a id="src-sparsepim-cpp"></a>

## src/SparsePIM.cpp

The controller: physical addresses, launch-time validation, memory requests, instruction execution, and synchronization. Its PC is a sparse microprogram state rather than an arbitrary CRF instruction address.

<a id="src-transaction-h"></a>

## src/Transaction.h

A transaction is a request submitted to memory. The callback field remembers how to notify the requester when it finishes.

**3 added, 0 removed lines.**

| Changed line | Exact source text | What it does and why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [+ L34](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/Transaction.h:34) | <code>#include &lt;functional&gt;</code> | Imports the C++ callback type std::function used by the new completion field. |
| [+ L63](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/Transaction.h:63) | <code>    // Optional device-owned completion; legacy transactions leave this empty.</code> | Documents that ordinary host transactions can leave the new callback empty. |
| [+ L64](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/Transaction.h:64) | <code>    std::function&lt;void()&gt; onComplete;</code> | Adds an optional function to invoke when this transaction finishes. void() means it takes no arguments and returns no result; it remembers the requesting block through a captured callback. |

<a id="src-buspacket-h"></a>

## src/BusPacket.h

A bus packet is the lower-level representation used by the memory system. The callback must survive conversion from a transaction to a packet.

**2 added, 0 removed lines.**

| Changed line | Exact source text | What it does and why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [+ L34](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/BusPacket.h:34) | <code>#include &lt;functional&gt;</code> | Imports std::function so lower-level memory packets can carry a completion callback. |
| [+ L68](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/BusPacket.h:68) | <code>    std::function&lt;void()&gt; onComplete;</code> | Keeps the transaction&#x27;s completion notification attached while it travels as a DRAM command/data packet. Without this, conversion into a packet would lose who to wake. |

## src/MemoryController.h

| [+ L72](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.h:72) | <code>    // Monotonic, independent of the existing epoch-reset statistics.</code> | Explains that these counters accumulate for the entire simulation rather than resetting at statistics-reporting intervals. |
| [+ L73](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.h:73) | <code>    struct Activity</code> | Introduces a small collection of memory-activity counters. |
| [+ L74](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.h:74) | <code>    {</code> | Opens the body of the function, type, loop, or condition named immediately above; the following indented statements belong to it. |

Separate counts let activation/refresh behavior affect energy, not just read/write traffic. |
| [+ L76](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.h:76) | <code>    } activity;</code> | Creates one Activity object in each memory controller, so every channel can report its own actual issued commands. |

<a id="src-memorycontroller-cpp"></a>

## src/MemoryController.cpp

Carries callbacks through the memory system’s existing representations, invokes them on actual completion, and counts issued commands. This preserves the timing path instead of directly reading bank storage.

**19 added, 1 removed lines.**

| Changed line | Exact source text | What it does and why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [+ L121](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:121) | <code>    returnTransaction.back()-&gt;onComplete = bpacket-&gt;onComplete;</code> | Preserves the completion identity when returned read data becomes a return transaction. This also helps match duplicate-address requests to their own buffers. |
| [+ L134](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:134) | <code>    if (trans-&gt;onComplete) trans-&gt;onComplete();</code> | After a read is returned, invokes its optional device callback so the requesting block can consume its operand and advance. |
| [+ L178](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:178) | <code>        writeDataToSend.back()-&gt;onComplete = poppedBusPacket-&gt;onComplete;</code> | Copies the callback from a write command to its later write-data packet. The write finishes with data delivery, not merely command issue. |
| [+ L188](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:188) | <code>    // Monotonic event counts for opt-in energy estimates; never reset at epochs.</code> | Documents why the new command counters survive periodic statistics resets; before/after subtraction must remain valid. |
| [+ L189](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:189) | <code>    switch (poppedBusPacket-&gt;busPacketType)</code> | Selects which issued DRAM command to count. This extra switch only measures activity; the existing switch below still implements timing. |
| [+ L190](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:190) | <code>    {</code> | Opens the body of the function, type, loop, or condition named immediately above; the following indented statements belong to it. |
| [+ L191](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:191) | <code>        case READ: ++activity.reads; break;</code> | Counts one issued read command and stops this counter-selection case. |
| [+ L192](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:192) | <code>        case WRITE: ++activity.writes; break;</code> | Counts one issued write command. |
| [+ L193](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:193) | <code>        case ACTIVATE: ++activity.activates; break;</code> | Counts opening a DRAM row, whose energy can differ between execution schedules. |
| [+ L194](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:194) | <code>        case PRECHARGE: ++activity.precharges; break;</code> | Counts closing/preparing a bank for another row, another source of schedule-dependent energy. |
| [+ L195](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:195) | <code>        case REF: case RFCSB: ++activity.refreshes; break;</code> | Counts either form of refresh in one category. The current estimator assigns both the same configurable event cost. |
| [+ L196](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:196) | <code>        default: break;</code> | Does not count command kinds outside these five categories. |
| [+ L197](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:197) | <code>    }</code> | Closes the preceding code block; this boundary has no separate simulated timing cost. |
| [+ L364](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:364) | <code>            command-&gt;onComplete = transaction-&gt;onComplete;</code> | Preserves the completion callback when the submitted transaction becomes an enqueued DRAM command. |
| [+ L501](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:501) | <code>            auto completed = outgoingDataPacket-&gt;onComplete;</code> | Makes a safe copy of the callback before delivering the packet, because the receiving rank can delete that packet. |
| [+ L504](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:504) | <code>            if (completed) completed();</code> | Invokes the copied callback after write data has been delivered, so the block sees its output as committed before advancing. |
| − old L551 | <code>            if (pendingReadTransactions[i]-&gt;address == returnTransaction[0]-&gt;address)</code> | Replaced address-only read matching with a condition that also checks destination-buffer identity for device callbacks, so same-address requests can wake the right block. Legacy requests keep their original rule. |
| [+ L567](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:567) | <code>            if (pendingReadTransactions[i]-&gt;address == returnTransaction[0]-&gt;address &amp;&amp;</code> | Still requires a matching physical address when associating returned read data with a waiting request. |
| [+ L568](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:568) | <code>                ((!pendingReadTransactions[i]-&gt;onComplete &amp;&amp; !returnTransaction[0]-&gt;onComplete) &#124;&#124;</code> | Keeps the original address-only matching rule when both requests have no device callback, preserving ordinary host behavior. |
| [+ L569](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/src/MemoryController.cpp:569) | <code>                 pendingReadTransactions[i]-&gt;data == returnTransaction[0]-&gt;data))</code> | For device-aware reads, also requires the same destination buffer. Two blocks reading the same address must not wake each other by mistake. |

Makes sparse execution part of the simulator clock and pending-work accounting, and adds guarded job submission.

The test does not choose a particular block or instruction. |

## data/sparse_matrices/pim_energy_example.txt

An explicit set of illustrative numbers for exercising the estimator. The numbers were not derived from hardware measurements; they must be replaced for hardware power claims.

## scripts/run_sparse_benchmarks.sh

Build support only: optional GoogleTest search paths for the executable and both libraries. No simulator timing flags were changed.

## results/independent_pim_host_baseline.csv

Saved outputs from all 30 original host-traffic cases. These lines do not implement behavior; they record baseline measurements and preserve their original schema.

<a id="results-independent-pim-paired-csv"></a>

## results/independent_pim_paired.csv

Saved outputs from 30 cases under each of the two control policies (60 measurements). Each policy pair uses matching inputs and validates simulated output.

**61 added, 0 removed lines.**

| Changed line | Exact source text | What it does and why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [+ L1](/home/evanh1122/eecs570/PIMSimulator/results/independent_pim_paired.csv:1) | <code>group,name,pattern,case_format,execution_format,system_config,rows,cols,nnz,control,seed,mac_latency_cycles,physical_blocks,cycles,runtime_ns,reads,writes,macs,memory_wait_block_cycles,barrier_block_cycles,max_row_lead,estimated_dram_energy_pj,estimated_pim_energy_pj,estimated_total_energy_pj,estimated_average_power_mw,energy_model</code> | Defines the meaning and ordering of every field in each following data row: group: case category; name: case identifier; pattern: deterministic generation pattern; case_format: requested format label from the original case list; execution_format: actual device layout, currently CSR for every PIM case; system_config: memory-system configuration file; rows: matrix/output row count; cols: matrix/input-vector column count; nnz: number of stored nonzero entries; control: lockstep baseline or independent control policy; seed: deterministic generation seed; mac_latency_cycles: modeled per-MAC latency; physical_blocks: total configured blocks, including idle blocks charged static power; cycles: elapsed simulated cycles (host traffic or sparse execution depending on the file); runtime_ns: execution cycles times DRAM clock period, in nanoseconds; reads: issued memory reads in the measured phase; writes: issued memory writes in the measured phase; macs: completed scalar multiply-accumulates; memory_wait_block_cycles: sum of cycles blocks wait for their outstanding memory requests; barrier_block_cycles: sum of cycles blocks wait for peers at barriers; max_row_lead: largest local-row progress gap within a rank; it can include differences in assigned row counts; estimated_dram_energy_pj: DRAM command-event and static-energy estimate in picojoules; estimated_pim_energy_pj: PIM MAC/instruction and static-energy estimate in picojoules; estimated_total_energy_pj: sum of DRAM and PIM energy estimates; estimated_average_power_mw: total estimated picojoules divided by elapsed nanoseconds, yielding milliwatts; energy_model: name of the coefficient assumptions used for this run. |

<a id="results-independent-pim-summary-csv"></a>

## results/independent_pim_summary.csv

Derived paired comparisons. These lines retain gains and regressions; negative energy/power reductions are increases, and speedups below one are slowdowns.

**31 added, 0 removed lines.**

## Coverage and scope of this walkthrough

The tables account for **1624 added lines + 9 removed lines = 1633 changed lines** in commit 3b98807, across **23 files**. Every added line has an explanation, and every removed line has a replacement rationale. Imported baseline code, comments, whitespace, documentation, CSV headers, and every saved CSV row are included.

The line numbers and exact source text were checked against the committed patch. This pass adds only this explanatory Markdown file. It does not change code, configuration, benchmark inputs, thresholds, or measured results, and it does not rerun performance experiments. The implementation guide records the original validation runs.
